# The SAI Observatory — native 3D inside GitHub

This is a **real, draggable 3D object rendered by GitHub itself**, not a video, screenshot, SVG, iframe, or JavaScript application.

Rotate it to explore a compact orbital sculpture: an event horizon with three accretion rings, three research satellites, and an extruded **SAI** wordmark.

The geometry is authored as a compact ASCII STL mesh and rendered by the native GitHub Markdown `stl` viewer. Try dragging the object to rotate it, scrolling to zoom, or switching the viewer to wireframe.

> **Experiment, not the public profile.** This is a proof-of-concept for a truly three-dimensional profile. The project links, research notes, accessibility, and primary text still need a readable Markdown layer.


## What the 3D viewer proves

- The geometry is truly three-dimensional and explorable without leaving GitHub.
- STL has no embedded colors, textures, materials, click targets, or runtime animation. It's best for the **visual centerpieces** of a profile, not navigation or long text.
- We could use multiple small STL viewers for projects, kernel diagrams, or research architectures, with short Markdown and hyperlinks between them.
- GitHub's README itself has a 500 KiB truncation limit, so the complete profile needs careful triangle and byte budgets.

[Open the same model in GitHub's standalone STL viewer](../models/sai-observatory.stl) · [Return to the existing profile](../README.md)

Generated for the native 3D profile experiment. No third-party scripts or model hosting are involved.
